# Code review: the GBPixelVfx plugin (first implementation by Muse Spark)

You are the reviewer. Read-only: do not edit, build or run anything. Project `C:\GameJam`, Unreal Engine 5.9.0 at `C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.9` (check engine APIs in its source when a finding depends on them).

## What the plugin is
`Glyphbound/Plugins/GBPixelVfx/`: bakes a Niagara / Niagara Fluids effect into a pixel-art flipbook in the game's palette (`Content/Data/Vfx/<Id>.png` + `.json`), and plays it at runtime on a camera-facing quad with a point light driven by a baked light track. The specification is `Prompts/pixelvfx_implement.md` plus `Prompts/pixelvfx_astra_notes.md` (the notes win where they differ). Bake jobs: `Glyphbound/Plugins/GBPixelVfx/Bake/Jobs/*.json`. Machine script: `Tools/Jam/PixelVfx-Jam.ps1`.

## What to review (in this order of importance)
1. `Source/GBPixelVfx/**` (the Runtime module: it ships in the packaged game). Crashes, GC safety of the transient texture / dynamic material / spawned components, behavior when a clip or the material is missing, packaged-build behavior (paths under `FPaths::ProjectContentDir()`, no Editor-only API, no dependency on the baker module or on Niagara), one-shot clean-up, the facing math and the pivot rule (the point `PivotU/V` of the frame must land on the component's location for every facing mode; the light must sit at `AnchorU/V`), frame selection, mirrored or upside-down sprites.
   The architect already suspects one bug: `UGBPixelVfxComponent::UpdateQuadTransform` applies the pivot offset (which is in the quad's own XY) through `SetRelativeLocation` in the parent's space before the quad is rotated, so for `Upright` / `Billboard` the offset goes sideways instead of up. Confirm or refute, and give the correct math.
2. `Source/GBPixelVfxBaker/Private/GBPixelVfxConvert.cpp` + `Public/GBPixelVfxConvert.h` (pure conversion): does each step do what the spec says (order: exposure, loop blend, downsample, alpha, levels, hard alpha, color mapping Ramp / Split / Nearest, hysteresis, islands / holes, outline, light track)? Premultiplied-color mistakes, percentile errors, off-by-one on ramp indices, hysteresis that can leave trails, loop blend direction, division by zero, out-of-range indices on odd sizes.
3. `Private/Tests/GBPixelVfxConvertTests.cpp`: for each test, name a wrong implementation that would still pass it (project rule: every test must be able to fail). Report only tests that are really too weak, and missing tests only for logic that is easy to break.
4. `Private/GBPixelVfxBakeJob.cpp`, `Private/GBPixelVfxBakeGameMode_Bake.cpp`: job parsing defaults vs the job files, capture set-up (fixed step, show-only list, alpha = 1 - A, auto-frame, frame counting, readback), hangs (every wait must have a frame budget), work done when no `-Pvfx...` switch is present (the cook's Editor process can load this module: it must be inert), file output paths.
5. `GBPixelVfx.uplugin`, both `Build.cs`, `Tools/make_pixelvfx_material.py`.
Do NOT review `Private/GBPixelVfxBakeGameMode_Stage.cpp` in depth (showcase / compare stage: still being changed by the implementer); only flag something there if it can crash the bake path or leak into the packaged game.

# Task: write the GBPixelVfx plugin (Niagara / Niagara Fluids -> pixel-art flipbooks with a light track)

You are the implementer. The architect (Claude Code) wrote this brief, the bake jobs and the machine script; you write the plugin. Work in `C:\GameJam`. Unreal Engine **5.9.0** at `C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.9` (newer than your training data: **grep the engine source for every API you are not sure of** before you use it).

## What it is for
The game (Glyphbound, Epic AI MegaJam 2026) draws all magic as pixel art in a fixed palette (`Design/VisualStyleGuide.md`). Fire, smoke and explosions are hard to draw by hand. This plugin takes a realistic Niagara effect, above all **Niagara Fluids** gas simulations, and bakes it into a pixel-art flipbook in the game's palette, which then plays at runtime for almost nothing and still **lights the scene** (a real point light driven by a baked light track, so Lumen / ray-traced shadows, GI and reflections keep reacting to it). It is not a pixelate filter: colors are mapped to palette ramps, alpha is hard, shapes are cleaned, flicker is damped.

## Read first
1. `CLAUDE.md` (project rules), `Design/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `Design/VisualStyleGuide.md`.
2. `Tools/Jam/PixelVfx-Jam.ps1` (the ONLY way you build, test, make the material, bake and run; it defines the command lines and the log markers your code must print).
3. `Glyphbound/Plugins/GBPixelVfx/Bake/Jobs/*.json` (the bake jobs: your loader reads exactly these keys).
4. `Tools/Jam/make_materials.py` (pattern for the material script), `Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/UI/GBPixelSpriteLibrary.cpp` (`CreateTexture`: how the game makes a transient nearest-filtered texture), `Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/Core/GBDevCommands.cpp` (how `gb.Smoke` takes screenshots and quits; read only).
5. `Prompts/pixelvfx_astra_notes.md` if it exists: a second architect's notes on this plan. Where they contradict this brief, the notes win.

## File ownership (hard rule)
You own ONLY `Glyphbound/Plugins/GBPixelVfx/**` (except `Bake/Jobs/*.json`, which are the architect's: report wanted changes, do not edit) and the outputs your runs write (`Glyphbound/Content/Data/Vfx/*`, `Glyphbound/Saved/PixelVfx/*`, `Glyphbound/Content/Glyphbound/Materials/M_PixelVfx*.uasset`).

`.uplugin`: FileVersion 3, `"EnabledByDefault": false`, `"CanContainContent": false`, modules `GBPixelVfx` (Runtime, Default) and `GBPixelVfxBaker` (UncookedOnly, Default), plugin dependency `Niagara`. Log category `LogGBPixelVfx` (defined in the runtime module, exported). ASCII only, comments in English, class prefix `GBPixelVfx`. Module files are unity-built: file-local helper names must be unique across files of a module. Never name a member `Cast`.

## Data: the baked clip (written by the baker, read by the runtime)
`Glyphbound/Content/Data/Vfx/<Id>.png` (RGBA8 atlas, frames left to right, top to bottom, alpha 0 or 255 only) and `<Id>.json`:
```json
{
  "Id": "FluidFire", "Atlas": "FluidFire.png",
  "FrameWidth": 48, "FrameHeight": 64, "Columns": 8, "FrameCount": 24, "Fps": 12, "Loop": true,
  "WorldWidthCm": 310.0, "WorldHeightCm": 413.3,
  "PivotU": 0.5, "PivotV": 0.94,
  "Facing": "Upright", "Glow": 1.5,
  "Light": { "Enabled": true, "Color": "#F3610B", "Intensity": 12.0, "RadiusCm": 900, "CastShadows": true,
             "AnchorU": 0.5, "AnchorV": 0.6, "Track": [0.82, 0.9, 1.0] },
  "Source": { "System": "/NiagaraFluids/...", "DurationSeconds": 2.0, "SimFps": 60, "EngineVersion": "5.9.0", "BakedUtc": "..." }
}
```
- `WorldWidthCm/HeightCm`: the real size of the captured area, so the baked sprite fills the same space as the source effect.
- `PivotU/V` (0..1, V down): where the source system's origin falls inside the frame. The component places the quad so that this point sits at the component's location: a clip spawned where the Niagara system was spawned overlaps it.
- `Light.Track`: one value 0..1 per frame (1 = the brightest frame of the clip). `AnchorU/V`: average emissive centroid, where the light sits inside the frame. `Intensity` is candelas at track value 1.
At runtime the folder is `FPaths::ProjectContentDir() / "Data/Vfx"` (it is staged as loose files in the packaged game, see `Glyphbound/Config/DefaultGame.ini`).

## Runtime module `GBPixelVfx`
- `FGBPixelVfxClip` (plain struct, `GBPixelVfxTypes.h`) with the fields above + `bool LoadFromJsonString / SaveToJsonString` + pure helpers that tests can call: `static FVector4f FrameRect(const FGBPixelVfxClip&, int32 FrameIndex)` (UV offset.xy, scale.zw of a frame in the atlas) and `static int32 FrameAtTime(const FGBPixelVfxClip&, float Seconds, bool& bOutFinished)` (stepped: floor(Seconds * Fps); loops wrap; one-shots clamp to the last frame and set bOutFinished after it has been shown for one frame duration).
- `EGBPixelVfxFacing`: `Billboard` (quad faces the camera fully), `Upright` (rotates around world Z only: fire, smoke columns), `Ground` (flat on the floor, facing +Z), `Fixed` (keeps the component's rotation).
- `UGBPixelVfxSubsystem : UGameInstanceSubsystem`: `const FGBPixelVfxClip* FindClip(FName Id)` and `UTexture2D* GetAtlas(FName Id)`; loads `<Id>.json` and decodes the PNG with `IImageWrapperModule` into a **transient** `UTexture2D` (PF_B8G8R8A8, SRGB true, `TF_Nearest`, no mips, `TA_Clamp`, `NeverStream`), cached; `ListClipIds()` (scans the folder); `ClearCache()`. Missing or broken clip: one warning, return null, never crash.
- `UGBPixelVfxComponent : USceneComponent`:
  - `UFUNCTION(BlueprintCallable) bool Play(FName ClipId, bool bRestart = true)`, `Stop()`, `IsPlaying()`, properties `bAutoDestroyOwnerWhenFinished`, `PlayRate`, `SizeScale`, `LightIntensityScale`, `bEnableLight` (default true), `bLightCastsShadows` (default from the clip), `FacingOverride` (optional), `StartTimeOffset`.
  - Creates at runtime (in `Play`, not in the constructor) a `UStaticMeshComponent` child with `/Engine/BasicShapes/Plane` (100 cm, XY, faces +Z; check its UV orientation and rotate/flip so the sprite is upright and not mirrored when seen by the camera), no collision, no shadow casting, movable, and a `UMaterialInstanceDynamic` of `/Game/Glyphbound/Materials/M_PixelVfxSprite` (params below). If the material is missing: one warning and fall back to `/Engine/BasicShapes/BasicShapeMaterial` so something still shows.
  - Each tick: frame from time (stepped, never interpolated), set `FrameRect`; orient the quad by facing mode using the first local player's camera (`UGameplayStatics::GetPlayerCameraManager`); when there is no player camera keep the last rotation. Quad size = clip world size * `SizeScale`; position so the pivot rule above holds.
  - Light: a `UPointLightComponent` child (movable, `IntensityUnits = ELightUnits::Candelas`, `SetAttenuationRadius(RadiusCm * SizeScale)`, color from the clip, `SetCastShadows(clip/flag)`), placed at the anchor point, `SetIntensity(Clip.Light.Intensity * Track[frame] * LightIntensityScale)` each time the frame changes. No light component at all when the clip has `Light.Enabled` false or `bEnableLight` is false.
  - Ray tracing / Lumen flags on the quad: `SetVisibleInRayTracing(true)`, `bAffectDynamicIndirectLighting = true`, `bVisibleInReflectionCaptures = true`, `CastShadow = false` (the quad must not shadow its own light).
- `AGBPixelVfxActor`: an actor whose root is the component; `static AGBPixelVfxActor* SpawnPixelVfx(UObject* WorldContext, FName ClipId, const FTransform& Transform, float SizeScale = 1.f)` (one-shot clips destroy the actor when finished; loops live until destroyed).
- Console command `pvfx.Spawn <ClipId> [SizeScale]` (non-shipping, registered by the runtime module): spawns the clip 300 cm in front of the first player's view, on the floor under that point if a line trace finds one. This is how a person looks at a clip in the real game.

## Material script `Tools/make_pixelvfx_material.py`
Same pattern and helpers as `Tools/Jam/make_materials.py` (copy what you need into your file; do not import or edit theirs). Log prefix `make_pixelvfx_material:`; the last line on success is `make_pixelvfx_material: OK`. It builds, in `/Game/Glyphbound/Materials` (already in `DirectoriesToAlwaysCook`):
- `M_PixelVfxSprite`: Masked, Unlit, two-sided. Params: `Atlas` (texture object, sampled with the texture's own sampler "from texture asset" so nearest filtering survives), `FrameRect` (vector: UV = TexCoord0 * FrameRect.zw + FrameRect.xy), `Tint` (vector, default white), `Glow` (scalar, default 0). Emissive = sample.rgb * Tint.rgb * (1 + Glow). Opacity mask = sample.a (clip value 0.5).
- `M_PixelVfxStage`: Opaque, Default Lit. Params `Color` (vector), `Roughness` (scalar, default 0.6), `Metallic` (scalar, default 0). Used only by the showcase stage (a glossy floor to see reflections).

## Baker module `GBPixelVfxBaker`

### Entry point
`PixelVfx-Jam.ps1` starts `UnrealEditor.exe <project> /Engine/Maps/Entry?game=/Script/GBPixelVfxBaker.GBPixelVfxBakeGameMode -game ... -EnablePlugins=GBPixelVfx,NiagaraFluids` plus ONE of `-PvfxBake=<Id|All>`, `-PvfxCompare=<Id>`, `-PvfxShowcase`.
`AGBPixelVfxBakeGameMode` (DefaultPawnClass = nullptr, HUDClass = nullptr, spectator off): reads the mode with `FParse::Value(FCommandLine::Get(), ...)`, runs it as a small state machine in `Tick`, and at the end logs **`PVFX DONE`** and quits (`FPlatformMisc::RequestExit(false)` or the console `quit`). Every progress line is `UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Display, TEXT("PVFX ..."))`; every problem is a `LogGBPixelVfx` Warning or Error (the script counts Errors as failure). A job that fails logs an Error and the run goes on to the next job. Guard against hangs: every wait has a frame budget, after which the job fails with an Error.

### Job loader (`FGBPixelVfxBakeJob`)
Reads `FPaths::ProjectPluginsDir() / "GBPixelVfx/Bake/Jobs/<Id>.json"` (All = every file there). Keys = exactly those in the job files (`Id`, `System`, `UserFloats`, `UserVectors`, `UserColors`, `Capture.*`, `Pixel.*`, `Light.*`, `World.*`); every key optional with the default that the FluidFire job shows; unknown keys ignored; `Comment` ignored. Colors are `#RRGGBB` sRGB strings. `Pixel.Ramps` is an array of ramps, each an array of colors from dark to light.

### Capture (per job)
1. Fixed time step for the whole process: `FApp::SetUseFixedTimeStep(true)`, `FApp::SetFixedDeltaTime(1.0 / SimFps)` (see the Astra notes for anything else).
2. Spawn an actor at the world origin with a `UNiagaraComponent`; `LoadObject<UNiagaraSystem>` the job's `System` (fail the job with an Error naming the path if it does not load: NiagaraFluids must be on the command line); apply user parameters (`SetVariableFloat`, `SetVariableVec3`, `SetVariableLinearColor`: check the 5.9 names); activate. Wait until the system has compiled and is ready (`UNiagaraSystem::IsReadyToRun` / `WaitForCompilationComplete`, check the names) before counting warm-up time.
3. Lighting for the capture: when `Capture.KeyLight` is true spawn a movable directional light coming from the **top left as seen by the capture camera** (style guide: light from the top left), intensity `KeyLightIntensity` (lux). No sky, no fog: the background must stay black.
4. A `USceneCaptureComponent2D`: **long-lens perspective** (`Capture.FovDeg`, default 10; see the notes, item 1; "Ortho" stays as an option with `OrthoWidth = WidthCm`), placed at `(WidthCm / 2) / tan(FovDeg / 2)` from `CenterCm` along the view direction given by `ViewYawDeg` / `ViewPitchDeg` (yaw 0 = the camera looks along +X, i.e. it sits at -X), `PrimitiveRenderMode = PRM_UseShowOnlyList` with the Niagara component in `ShowOnlyComponents`, `CaptureSource = SCS_SceneColorHDR` (RGB = scene color, A = inverse opacity), `bCaptureEveryFrame = false`, `bCaptureOnMovement = false`, `bAlwaysPersistRenderingState = true`, auto exposure / bloom / motion blur / tone mapper effects off in its show flags and post-process settings so frames are comparable. Render target `RTF_RGBA16f`, `Resolution` px wide, height = Resolution * FrameHeight / FrameWidth.
5. **Auto-frame pre-pass** (when `Capture.AutoFrame`): run the system once (warm-up + duration) capturing every 4th simulation frame at 256 px with the job's `WidthCm` / `CenterCm` as a generous first guess; compute the union bounding box (in world cm on the capture plane) of pixels whose alpha (rule below) is above `AlphaThreshold / 2`; expand by `AutoFramePadding` (fraction of the box size per side); keep the frame aspect (grow the short side); if the box touches the capture edge, log a Warning ("first guess too tight") and double `WidthCm` once and retry. Then destroy and re-spawn the system and do the real pass with that framing. If nothing is visible at all: Error "nothing visible", fail the job.
6. Real pass: wait `WarmupSeconds` of simulated time, then capture `FrameCount = round(DurationSeconds * Pixel.Fps)` frames, one every `SimFps / Fps` simulation frames: `CaptureScene()`, then read back with `FTextureRenderTargetResource::ReadLinearColorPixels` (or `ReadFloat16Pixels`; blocking is fine). Keep the raw `FLinearColor` frames in memory.
7. Per pixel: `Opacity = saturate(1 - A)`. Keep RGB as captured (linear HDR, already premultiplied by coverage against black).
8. Destroy the job's actors before the next job.

### Conversion (`GBPixelVfxConvert`, pure functions over `TArray<FLinearColor>` frames: unit-testable, no engine objects)
In this order:
1. **Exposure:** RGB *= `Capture.Exposure`.
2. **Loop blend** (only when `Loop` and `LoopBlendFrames` > 0, on the full-resolution linear frames): capture `LoopBlendFrames` EXTRA frames after the last one (so the baker really captures FrameCount + LoopBlendFrames); for i in [0, B): frame[i] = lerp(extra[i], frame[i], (i + 0.5) / B) on RGB and opacity. The extra frames are then dropped: the clip's last frame flows into the first.
3. **Downsample** each frame to `FrameWidth x FrameHeight` by box average (RGB and opacity).
4. **Alpha:** per pixel `Lum = dot(RGB, (0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722))`; `AlphaMode` "Opacity": a = Opacity; "Luminance": a = saturate(Lum); "Max": a = max(Opacity, saturate(Lum)). Un-premultiply the color for the color step: `C = RGB / max(a, 1e-4)`.
5. **Levels:** `Peak` = the `LevelsPercentile` percentile of `Lum(C)` over all visible pixels (a >= AlphaThreshold) of ALL frames (one value per clip, so the clip does not pump); value `v = saturate(pow(Lum(C) / Peak, Gamma))`.
6. **Hard alpha:** pixel is solid when a >= `AlphaThreshold`, else fully transparent. No partial alpha, no dithering.
7. **Color mapping** to the palette:
   - "Ramp": index = floor(v * N) clamped, in ramp 0.
   - "Split": a pixel is HOT when its un-premultiplied HDR luminance (before levels) >= `HotLuminance` OR its saturation ((max-min)/max of C) >= `HotSaturation`; hot pixels use ramp 0 with v re-normalized among hot pixels (own percentile peak), the others use ramp 1 with v re-normalized among cold pixels. If the job has only one ramp, behave as "Ramp".
   - "Nearest": nearest color among all colors of all ramps, distance in OKLab, after tone-mapping C with `C / (1 + C)` scaled by 1/Peak.
8. **Temporal hysteresis:** when mapping frame f > 0, if the pixel was solid in frame f-1 with ramp index k (same ramp), and the new v lies within `Hysteresis` of the boundary between k and the new index, keep k. (Implement it as: the band of the previous index is widened by `Hysteresis` on both sides.) For a looping clip also run frame 0 against the last frame in a second pass.
9. **Cleanup:** per frame, remove 4-connected solid islands smaller than `MinIslandPixels`; when `FillHoles`, a transparent pixel whose 4 neighbors are all solid becomes solid with the most common neighbor color.
10. **Outline** `"None" | "Black" | "Darkest"`: transparent pixels 4-adjacent to a solid pixel become `#000000` or the darkest color of ramp 0. Never draw outside the frame.
11. **Light track:** per frame, `E = sum over solid HOT pixels (all solid pixels when the mode is not Split) of v`; track = E / max(E over frames); anchor = average over frames of the v-weighted centroid of those pixels, as U,V in 0..1. If the job's `Light.Enabled` is false, no track.
12. **Pivot:** project the system origin (world 0,0,0 of the job actor) on the capture plane -> `PivotU/V`.
Output colors are the palette colors exactly (sRGB bytes from the hex strings), alpha 0 or 255.

### Outputs of a bake
- `Content/Data/Vfx/<Id>.png` and `<Id>.json` (the clip; `Columns` = ceil(sqrt(FrameCount)) capped so the atlas stays <= 1024 px wide).
- Review files in `Glyphbound/Saved/PixelVfx/<Id>/`: `sheet.png` = every baked frame, upscaled x6 with nearest, on a mid-grey `#3A3A3A` background, 8 per row with 6 px gutters; `source.png` = the same frames before conversion (after exposure, tone-mapped with x/(1+x), composited on black, each scaled to the same cell size, smooth) so a person can compare source and result side by side; `frame_big.png` = the brightest frame upscaled x12.
- Log per job: `PVFX BAKE <Id>: frames=<n> frame=<w>x<h> world=<w>x<h> cm peak=<Peak> solid=<average % of solid pixels> colors=<distinct colors used> -> <png path>`.
- A job whose average solid coverage is below 1% or above 95% logs a Warning (framing or threshold is wrong).

### Showcase mode (`-PvfxShowcase`)
Builds a small stage from engine basic shapes: a floor 3000 x 2000 cm (dynamic instance of `M_PixelVfxStage`, dark, roughness 0.15 so reflections show), a back wall (roughness 0.7, grey), three boxes / cylinders of different heights standing between the clips and the wall so the clip lights throw visible shadows, one very dim directional light (so the stage is readable but the clip lights dominate), no sky. Spawns every clip found by `UGBPixelVfxSubsystem::ListClipIds()` in a row 600 cm apart, on the floor (`AGBPixelVfxActor`; one-shots are re-spawned when they finish). Spawns a camera (a `ACameraActor` set as the player controller's view target; if the game mode has no player controller, spawn one) that sees the whole row. Waits 2 s (real frames, not fixed step: restore `FApp::SetUseFixedTimeStep(false)` in this mode), takes `Saved/PixelVfx/Showcase/showcase_1.png`, then 0.7 s later `showcase_2.png`, then a close shot of each clip `showcase_<Id>.png`, using `FScreenshotRequest::RequestScreenshot(Path, false, false)` and waiting a few frames after each request (see how `gb.Smoke` does it). Logs `PVFX SHOWCASE: <n> clips, screenshots in <dir>`, then `PVFX DONE`.

### Compare mode (`-PvfxCompare=<Id>`): the "it is lighter" number
Same stage. Phase 0: one source Niagara system (from the job) on the left and the baked clip on the right, 500 cm apart; after the job's warm-up + 1 s take `Saved/PixelVfx/<Id>/compare.png`. Phase A: destroy both, spawn 8 instances of the SOURCE system in a 4 x 2 grid in view; let them run 3 s; then for 180 frames measure the real frame time (`FPlatformTime::Seconds()` between ticks; make sure nothing caps the frame rate: `t.MaxFPS 0`, `r.VSync 0`, fixed time step off) and the GPU frame time (`RHIGetGPUFrameCycles` -> ms; check the 5.9 name). Phase B: destroy them, spawn 8 baked clips at the same places, 1 s, measure 180 frames. Phase C: empty stage, 1 s, measure 180 frames (the baseline). Log one line:
`PVFX COMPARE <Id>: x8 source frame=<ms> gpu=<ms> | x8 baked frame=<ms> gpu=<ms> | empty frame=<ms> gpu=<ms>` and write the same numbers to `Saved/PixelVfx/<Id>/compare.json`. Then `PVFX DONE`.

## Tests (`Private/Tests/GBPixelVfxConvertTests.cpp`, names `GBPixelVfx.<Area>.<Name>`, no name a prefix of another)
Pure logic only, each must be able to fail (ask: which wrong implementation would still pass?). Six to eight tests, for example:
- hard alpha: an input with alpha just below / above the threshold gives exactly 0 / 255, and no output pixel ever has another alpha;
- ramp mapping: a horizontal luminance gradient gives non-decreasing ramp indices that use the first and the last color; every output color is one of the ramp's colors;
- split mode: a bright saturated pixel lands in ramp 0 and a grey dim one in ramp 1;
- hysteresis: a pixel whose value oscillates across a band edge by less than the margin keeps one color over 6 frames, and by clearly more than the margin changes;
- islands and holes: a 2-pixel island is removed when MinIslandPixels is 3 and kept when it is 2; a one-pixel hole is filled;
- outline: a solid 2x2 block in a 6x6 frame gets exactly its 8 4-adjacent pixels outlined, none outside the frame for a block at the corner;
- loop blend: with B blend frames, frame 0 of the result equals (mostly) the extra frame, and the last frame is unchanged;
- clip helpers: `FrameRect` for frame 9 of an 8-column atlas, `FrameAtTime` wrap for loops and finish for one-shots; JSON round trip of a clip keeps the light track.

## Order of work (stop after each step if it fails; fix before going on)
1. `.uplugin`, both `Build.cs`, module files, types, convert functions, tests -> `-Action Build`, `-Action Test` green.
2. Runtime subsystem, component, actor, material script -> `-Action Build`, `-Action Materials`.
3. Job loader, bake game mode (bake mode) -> `-Action Bake -Id FluidFire`. Open `Saved/PixelVfx/FluidFire/sheet.png` and `source.png` yourself (you can read images): if the source is black or empty, the capture is wrong (show-only list, alpha, exposure, warm-up, framing): fix it before anything else. Then `FluidSmoke`, `FluidExplosion`, `FluidArcaneFire`.
4. Showcase mode -> `-Action Showcase`; look at the screenshots.
5. Compare mode -> `-Action Compare -Id FluidFire`.
If a step fights you for more than about 20 minutes (for example the fluid does not render in a scene capture), write down exactly what you tried and what the log says, pick the fallback the notes give (perspective camera, no show-only list with the stage far from everything, luminance alpha), and go on: a working bake of ANY Niagara Fluids template beats a perfect plan.

## Final report (your last message, plain text)
1. Files written. 2. The verbatim result lines of the last Build, Test, Materials, each Bake, Showcase and Compare. 3. What differs from this brief and why. 4. Job values you would change (you may not edit the job files). 5. What you could not verify, and anything that looked wrong in the review images.
# GBPixelVfx: notes from the plan review (Astra, GPT-6-Astra medium) and the architect's decision on each

Full answer: `Devlog/raw/CENTRAL/2026-11-13/agents/062728-codex-7ba49d/output.txt`. Astra checked the engine source read-only; it could not open `.uasset` files, so everything about how the Niagara Fluids materials behave is still **unverified until the first bake**.
Where a note contradicts `Prompts/pixelvfx_implement.md`, this file wins.

1. **Camera: long-lens perspective, not orthographic** (the fluid ray-march material uses the camera position; ortho is unverified). Decision: the capture uses a perspective `USceneCaptureComponent2D` with a narrow FOV (job key `Capture.FovDeg`, default 10) placed at `Distance = (WidthCm / 2) / tan(FovDeg / 2)` from `CenterCm`; `Capture.DistanceCm` in the jobs is ignored in this mode. `Capture.Projection` = "Perspective" (default) or "Ortho" (kept as an option to try).
   - Scene captures do NOT advance GPU simulations (`FSceneView::AllowGPUParticleUpdate()` excludes them): only the main view does. So the main offscreen game view must render every frame and must look at the effect: place the player's view (a camera actor set as view target) at the same position and direction as the capture camera, with the Niagara component visible to it.
   - The capture may see the simulation state of the previous frame. That is fine: capture at a consistent point of the frame (end of the game mode's Tick) and count frames, never wall time.
   - Show-only lists do work with Niagara mesh renderers (`SceneCaptureRendering.cpp::GetShowOnlyAndHiddenComponents`). If the fluid still comes out empty with the show-only list, the fallback is `PRM_LegacySceneCapture` (render everything; the bake world is empty anyway).
2. **Fixed time step from the command line**: the script now passes `-UseFixedTimeStep -FPS=60` for bakes (the delta of the first frame is chosen before BeginPlay). Keep the `FApp` calls too when a job asks for a `SimFps` other than 60. Before activating the system: `Component->SetRandomSeedOffset(0)` (a fixed value), `Component->SetAllowScalability(false)`, so the bake is not culled or scaled down. Do not promise bit-identical results between runs: the auto-frame pre-pass and the real pass are two simulations, so the auto-frame padding matters.
3. **UncookedOnly + Default loading phase is right.** The cook's Editor process can load the baker module too: the game mode and the module startup must do nothing unless a `-Pvfx...` switch is present. The runtime module must not reference any baker class.
4. **Plugin isolation works** (`-EnablePlugin=` for UBT, `-EnablePlugins=` at runtime), Editor target only. In the `.uplugin`, restrict the Niagara dependency to Editor targets: `"Plugins": [{ "Name": "Niagara", "Enabled": true, "TargetAllowList": ["Editor"] }]`. Never list NiagaraFluids there.
5. **Alpha**: keep the single pass with A = inverse opacity; `AlphaMode` stays per job ("Opacity", "Luminance", "Max"). Additive blending also reduces A in UE's translucency pass, so "Opacity" may already work for fire; look at the first bake and choose.
6. **Lighting wording and options.** The light track is a *stylized lighting approximation*, not the volume's real light transport. Direct shadows come from Virtual Shadow Maps (or ray-traced shadows when hardware RT is on); Lumen does GI and reflections. Decisions:
   - Hardware RT is NOT enabled in the project. The script's `-HardwareRT` switch runs one process with `-dx12 -ini:Engine:[/Script/Engine.RendererSettings]:r.RayTracing=1,r.Lumen.HardwareRayTracing=1`.
   - The Unlit emissive quad is visible to Lumen cards and to ray-tracing hit shaders as it is. Keep `CastShadow = false` on the quad.
   - New: a second sprite material `M_PixelVfxSpriteLit` (Masked, **Default Lit**, two-sided, base color = sample * Tint, roughness 1, specular 0, emissive = sample * Tint * Glow, same params) for effects that do not emit light: smoke. Clip / job key `World.Lit` (bool, default false; clip JSON key `Lit`). A lit pixel sprite receives the scene's light and shadows, including the light of a pixel fire next to it.
   - `Light.TrackMode`: "Baked" (default: the measured envelope) or "Constant" (always 1).
7. **Conversion**: the order in the brief already downsamples linear radiance before any tone mapping; keep it so. Captured RGB over black is already premultiplied: never multiply it by alpha again. Hysteresis only applies when the pixel was solid in the previous frame (already the rule); additionally skip it for a frame whose solid pixel count differs from the previous frame's by more than 30% (fast-changing frames: explosions). Island removal and hole filling default OFF in code (`MinIslandPixels` 0, `FillHoles` false); the jobs decide. Loop cross-blend only when `Loop` is true. Framing and pivot are fixed for the whole clip.
8. **Compare mode**: wall-clock time only (never `FApp::GetDeltaTime` under a fixed step), frame caps and VSync off, warm up each population before measuring, no readbacks or screenshots inside a measured window, and the baked population includes its lights (that is the honest cost). Report it as a quick benchmark, not as proof.
   - PNG decode: `IImageWrapper::GetRawImage` (5.9 recommends it over the format/depth overloads). Check the result and the dimensions (atlas must equal Columns * FrameWidth by rows * FrameHeight); set filter / sRGB / addressing before the final `UpdateResource`; keep the texture and the dynamic material in `UPROPERTY` references so GC does not take them.
   - One-shot clips: when finished, destroy the mesh and light components explicitly (or the owner actor).
   - `ReadLinearColorPixels` needs a real RHI (never `-NullRHI` for bakes).

## Tests y verificaciÃ³n
Reglas que salen de lo que nos costÃ³ horas en el proyecto anterior. El porquÃ© y las cifras estÃ¡n en `Devlog/PREJAM_PREP.md`, secciÃ³n "Lessons from our previous project: tests and verification time"; lÃ©ela antes de escribir un test o una herramienta de Editor.
1. **Pocos tests, y solo de lÃ³gica pura** que sea fÃ¡cil de romper y difÃ­cil de ver jugando (puntuaciÃ³n, guardado, mÃ¡quinas de estado, generadores, matemÃ¡ticas). Toda la suite por debajo de 30 s. Lo demÃ¡s se verifica jugando el juego empaquetado.
2. **Verde o rojo.** Nada de listas de "fallos conocidos", tests excluidos ni catÃ¡logos. Un test en rojo se arregla o se borra el mismo dÃ­a.
3. **Cada test tiene que poder fallar:** antes de darlo por bueno, pregÃºntate quÃ© implementaciÃ³n incorrecta lo pasarÃ­a igual. No escribas tests que copian constantes, miran el texto del cÃ³digo o comprueban que un campo existe.
4. **Corridas baratas:** filtros anclados (`^Game.Save.`, con `^` y punto final), varios filtros unidos con `+` en una sola corrida, y un mapa inicial del Editor casi vacÃ­o (no el nivel de juego). El nombre de un test nunca puede ser tambiÃ©n la carpeta de otro (`A.B` junto a `A.B.C`): Unreal deja de correr `A.B` sin avisar.
5. **Mide antes de optimizar:** arranque del Editor, suma de duraciones de los tests y tiempo total. Mide el conjunto, no una parte, y compara sobre el mismo cÃ³digo.
6. **Nada puede costar en proporciÃ³n a todo lo cargado:** ni una llamada de herramienta ni un test. No uses `CollectGarbage(RF_NoFlags)` en un test (descarga los assets; usa `GARBAGE_COLLECTION_KEEPFLAGS`), no guardes una cachÃ© por tamaÃ±o y fecha de archivo (Unreal da la fecha con precisiÃ³n de un segundo), y usa referencias blandas en el Blueprint del jugador para lo que no haga falta al aparecer.
7. **UE 5.9.0 fijo hasta entregar.** Si algo falla sin que haya cambiado el cÃ³digo, compara primero la versiÃ³n del motor en los dos logs.

Scripts de un solo uso: en un archivo, no en lÃ­nea dentro de un comando; en ASCII o con BOM si son `.ps1` (PowerShell 5.1 lee como ANSI un script sin BOM); y en JavaScript, para reemplazos literales usa `split(a).join(b)`, no `replace(a, b)`.

# Glyphbound: code architecture

> Written by the architect (Claude Code) on 2026-11-12. Source of truth for how the C++ is organized.
> Design lives in `Design/GDD.md`; this file says how the code implements it. Keep it short and current.

## Principles
1. **Data, not code.** Every gameplay number lives in `Glyphbound/Content/Data/*.json` and is read through `UGBDataSubsystem`. Code never hardcodes damage, costs, speeds, durations, sizes or timings. The JSON ships as loose files, so numbers can be tuned in a packaged build with a text editor (`gb.ReloadData` reloads while playing).
2. **One stat system.** A number is a *named stat*: base value from data, then modifiers. `UGBStatsComponent` evaluates `(Base + adds) * multipliers` (or an override). Skill-tree nodes, armor, status effects, air passives and the weapon enchant are all just groups of modifiers with a source name. Nothing else changes numbers.
3. **One damage path.** Everything that hurts goes through `UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(Target, FGBDamageInfo)`. Shields, resistances, teams, on-hit effects and kill credit hook in there once.
4. **Spells are data + a behavior.** A spell is a `FGBSpellDefinition` (params with `Base/Min/Max/ScaledBy`) plus the class of an `AGBSpellActor` behavior. The QTE result (power, reach) scales the params tagged for it. A new spell that fits an existing behavior is a JSON entry, no code.
5. **Shared pieces.** One projectile (`AGBProjectile`) for staff bolts, spell projectiles, enemy shots and grenades. One status-effect component for slows, burns, potions and buffs. One interact interface for portals, chests, pickups and the seal.
6. **Text in git.** No binary assets are required to run: input actions, the test arena, placeholder shapes and placeholder pixel sprites are all built in C++ from text. `Content/` is ignored by git except `Content/Data/`.
7. **No GAS.** Unreal's Gameplay Ability System was considered and rejected for the jam: it needs a lot of boilerplate and asset setup, and the game's needs (costs, cooldowns, timed effects, stat upgrades) are covered by principles 2-4 in a few hundred lines we fully control.
8. **PIE is human-only.** Agents build, run the pure-logic tests and package; a person plays.

## Module layout (`Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound`, one runtime module, prefix `GB`)
Includes are relative to the module root: `#include "Stats/GBStatsComponent.h"`.

| Folder | What | Key types |
|---|---|---|
| `Core/` | Shared enums, stat names, placeholders (plain shapes and pixel-textured structures), game mode, dev commands | `GBTypes.h`, `GBStatNames.h`, `GBPlaceholder.h` (`SetupTexturedShape`), `AGBGameMode`, `UGBGameInstance`, `GBDevCommands.cpp` (`gb.Smoke`) |
| `Data/` | Definitions and the JSON loader | `GBDataTypes.h`, `UGBDataSubsystem` |
| `Stats/` | Stats + modifiers, health/mana | `UGBStatsComponent`, `UGBAttributeComponent` |
| `Combat/` | Damage (one path, one global `OnDamageDealt` event), status effects, elemental build-up, projectile, explosion shockwave | `UGBCombatLibrary`, `UGBStatusEffectComponent`, `UGBElementalStatusComponent`, `FGBBuildUpMeter` (pure, tested), `AGBProjectile`, `AGBExplosionEffect` |
| `Player/` | Character, controller, input | `AGBPlayerCharacter`, `AGBPlayerController`, `UGBInputSet` |
| `Weapons/` | Held items and slots | `UGBEquipmentComponent`, `AGBHeldItem`, `AGBStaff`, `AGBGun`, `AGBSword` |
| `Spells/` | Spellbook, cast resolution, behaviors | `UGBSpellbookComponent`, `FGBSpellCast`, `AGBSpellActor`, `AGBSpell_*` |
| `QTE/` | Quick-time event | `FGBQTESession`, `FGBGestureRecognizer`, `UGBQTEComponent` |
| `World/` | Eras, progress, interactables, test arena | `UGBEraSubsystem`, `UGBEraComponent`, `UGBProgressSubsystem`, `IGBInteractable`, `AGBPortal`, `AGBChest`, `AGBPickup`, `AGBCheckpointVolume`, `AGBTestArena` |
| `AI/` | Enemies (navmesh path, local steering fallback, stuck check) and encounters | `AGBEnemyCharacter`, `GBSteering` (pure decision, tested), `AGBEncounter` |
| `Items/` | Inventory, consumables, armor, recipes | `UGBInventoryComponent` |
| `Progression/` | Skill trees | `UGBSkillTreeComponent` |
| `UI/` | Canvas HUD (two skins), menus, pixel sprites | `AGBHUD`, `UGBPixelSpriteLibrary` |
| `Tests/` | Pure-logic automation tests | `Glyphbound.<Area>.<Name>` |

## Data files (`Glyphbound/Content/Data`)
`Player.json`, `QTE.json`, `Game.json`, `Spells.json`, `Weapons.json`, `StatusEffects.json`, `Items.json` (items + recipes), `Enemies.json`, `Encounters.json`, `Skills.json`, `Sprites.json`. Schemas are the structs in `Data/GBDataTypes.h` (field name = JSON key). Stat and parameter names are the constants in `Core/GBStatNames.h`.

Modifier keys: an actor stat (`MoveSpeed`), `Weapon.<Id>.<Stat>`, `Weapon.<Slot>.<Stat>` (`Weapon.Staff`, `Weapon.Gun`, `Weapon.Sword`), `Weapon.All.<Stat>`, `Spell.<Id>.<Param>`, `Spell.All.<Param>`, `QTE.TimeDilation`...

Standard status effect ids (defined in `StatusEffects.json`, referenced from other files): `Slow`, `Ignite`, `HealthPotion`, `ManaPotion`, `HealthInjection`, `Enchant_Fire`, `Enchant_Lightning`, `Enchant_Ice`. (`Burn`, `Shock`, `Chill` were removed in GDD v0.22.)

Modifier ops: `Add`, `Multiply`, `Override`, and `MultiplyMin` (only the lowest one on a stat counts: slows never stack, the strongest wins).

Elemental keys (GDD 6 "Elemental build-up"): per enemy in `Enemies.json` `Stats` (`<E>DamageTaken`, `<E>BuildUpTaken`, `<E>BuildUpThreshold`, `<E>BuildUpDecayDelay`, `<E>BuildUpDecayRate`, `<E>StatusDuration`, `<E>StatusImmunity`, `IgniteDamageMult`, `ShockDischargeDamage`, `FrostSlow`; E = Fire, Lightning, Ice) and `Strings` (`<E>StatusReaction`); missing keys fall back to `Game.json` `ElementalDefault.<Key>`. Sources: spell param `BuildUp`, weapon stats `FireBuildUp` / `LightningBuildUp` / `IceBuildUp` and `WeakPointBuildUpMult`, item stat `BuildUp`.

## How the main flows run
- **Cast:** input -> `UGBSpellbookComponent::TryCastSlot` (checks staff in hand, known, cooldown, mana; spends mana) -> `UGBQTEComponent::StartQTE` (slow time, sequence = power, gesture = reach; or instant when off) -> `GBSpell::ResolveCast` (data -> QTE scaling -> modifiers) -> spawn the behavior actor -> it deals damage through `UGBCombatLibrary`.
- **Shoot:** input -> `AGBGun` (fire interval, magazine, spread, recoil, pellets; every number via `GetStat`) -> hitscan -> headshot and distance falloff math in `UGBCombatLibrary` -> `DealDamage`.
- **Era travel:** `AGBPortal::Interact` -> `UGBEraSubsystem::RequestTravel` (fade out, swap era for `UGBEraComponent` actors and `_Past` / `_Future` sub-levels, teleport, restore health/mana/ammo, save checkpoint, fade in).
- **Progress:** encounters set flags in `UGBProgressSubsystem` when cleared; `UGBEraComponent` reads flags, which is how era-gated zones and past-to-future unlocks are built. Death restores the last checkpoint snapshot; flags are never rolled back (enemies do not respawn).
- **Elemental build-up:** a hit carries `FGBDamageInfo::BuildUp` (spell param, weapon stat by element, item stat) and a `BuildUpEventId` (one per shot, cast, blast or tick). `DealDamage` passes it to the target's `UGBElementalStatusComponent` (`ApplyBuildUp`; the ice floor, which deals no damage, calls it directly), which dedupes by event id, applies the enemy's resistance and fills one of three `FGBBuildUpMeter`s. A full bar starts the status: Ignite is the `Ignite` status effect (DOT through `DealDamage`); Shock deals its discharge; Frost slow is a `MultiplyMin` modifier; stun, interrupt and freeze are done by `AGBEnemyCharacter` from the component's `OnStatusStarted` event. The component only ticks (every `ElementalTickInterval`) while a bar is not empty. Looks bind to its events; `gb.ShowElemental 1` is a debug view and `gb.BuildUp` a test command.
- **Upgrades:** `UGBSkillTreeComponent::Unlock` adds the node's modifiers to the player's stats; weapons and spells pick them up on their next read.

## Conventions
- Game text in English. Code comments in English. ASCII only in source files.
- Native multicast delegates (C++ project; no Blueprint graphs needed).
- No custom collision channels: projectiles and queries use the default channels and filter by team in code.
- Pointers to definitions are not kept across frames (data can be reloaded): keep the id, look it up.
- Tests: only pure logic that is easy to break and hard to see by playing; each one must be able to fail; names `Glyphbound.<Area>.<Name>` where a name is never also a prefix of another.
- Build: `powershell -File Tools/Jam/Build-Jam.ps1` (Editor closed). One build at a time per machine.
- Art rule in code: characters, items and effects are camera-facing pixel sprites; **structures are 3D shapes with pixel tiles** (`GBPlaceholder::SetupTexturedShape`, tiles are sprites of `Sprites.json` named `tile_*`). The material behind it (`M_PixelSurface`) is the only binary asset and is generated by `Tools/Jam/Make-Materials.ps1`, never edited by hand.
- A structure that looks different in each era has ONE hidden collision body present in both eras and two non-colliding looks (PastOnly / FutureOnly), so footing and navigation never change on era travel.
- UI that reacts to combat listens to `UGBCombatLibrary::OnDamageDealt()`; nothing in combat code knows about the HUD.
# Pixel-art visual style guide

> **Source.** The 9 sprites this guide is built from (`valki`, `axe`, `spada`, `sscudo`, `sscudo adnto`, `baston1`, `baston1.2`, `baston2`, `baston3`) were drawn **by hand in Pixelorama on the LAPTOP on 2026-11-12**, during the jam (file times 12:45 to 21:32 local, UTC-4). The guide was drafted the same evening by Cursor on the LAPTOP, which read the `.pxo` files, measured them (canvas, colors, alpha) and wrote it in Spanish at the artist's request. This is the English translation, made by the architect on CENTRAL; the Spanish original and the `.pxo` sources are in `Glyphbound/SourceArt/PixelArt/` (see its README). Log: `Devlog/raw/LAPTOP/2026-11-13/transcripts/cursor-2e46aba0-*.jsonl`.
>
> It complements GDD Â§3 "Art rule": that section says **what** is pixel art; this guide says **how** it is drawn. Where they disagree, the GDD wins.

---

## 1. The style in one line

> **Dark fantasy hi-bit pixel art, 16-bit inspired.**

- **Hi-bit:** modern pixel art that keeps the retro language (hard pixels, outline, a small palette per sprite) without a console's limits: large sprites, more colors, fine detail.
- **16-bit inspired:** the SNES / Mega Drive tradition: ramp shading, a dark outline, pinpoint highlights.
- **Dark fantasy:** dark base materials (iron, obsidian) with saturated accents (blood, fire, arcane, ice).

Tone references (reference only, nothing copied): *Castlevania: Symphony of the Night*, *Blasphemous*, *Eitr*, *Children of Morta*.

---

## 2. Visual pillars

1. **Silhouette first.** Every object reads from its black shape alone: crescent axe, straight sword, round shield, staff with an ornate head.
2. **Dark material + bright accent.** Most of a sprite is muted. Saturated color is kept for what is magical or important: gems, orbs, blades, hair.
3. **Clean pixels.** No semi-transparency and no automatic anti-aliasing. Every current sprite already follows this (alpha is 0 or 255).
4. **Few ramps, well chosen.** Each material has a fixed ramp; no new tones invented sprite by sprite.

---

## 3. Format and scale

| Rule | Value |
|---|---|
| Asset canvas (weapons, shields, characters) | 100Ã—100 px |
| Longest side of the sprite | 88â€“96 px |
| Minimum margin to the canvas edge | 2 px |
| Resolution | 1:1, no scaling inside the canvas |
| Transparency | 0% or 100% only |
| Animation frame rate | 6 fps as a base (what the files already use) |

**To decide:** the character (`valki`, 93 px) and the weapons (89â€“99 px) are almost the same height today. That works for inventory icons or equipment cards, not for weapons held in the game. Define two scales:

- **Icon scale:** the current one, the object filling the canvas.
- **World scale:** the weapon in proportion to the character's hand. For example, a sword of about 40â€“50 px for a 93 px character.

`axe` is 99 px tall and touches the canvas edge; shrink or re-center it to keep the margin.

---

## 4. Outline

- **Outer outline:** `#000000`, 1 px, all around. It is already the outline color of `valki`, `axe`, `spada` and the shields.
- **Inner outline** (between parts, such as blade and guard or strap and metal): the darkest tone of the material's ramp, not black.
- **Staffs:** today their darkest tone is `#1D1D25` and they have no black outline. Pick one option and apply it to all four:
  - Add a `#000000` outline outside, to match the rest (recommended).
  - Keep them without black and record it as the exception of the "obsidian" family.
- **Thickness:** always 1 px. Avoid doubled corners (a 2 px outline on diagonals) unless weight is wanted on purpose.

---

## 5. Light and shading

- **Light source:** top left. The highlights on the shield and the sword blade already follow it; apply it to everything.
- **Ramp per material:** 3 to 5 tones (deep shadow, shadow, base, light, specular).
- **Specular highlight:** 1 to 3 near-white pixels, only on polished metal, gems and orbs. Never on wood, cloth or stone.
- **Hue-shifted ramps:** shadows lean cold (blue or violet), lights lean warm (yellow). It brings life to dark fantasy and avoids "dirty" greys.
- **Texture:** the loose-pixel noise on the shield works as wear. Use it only on rough materials (old iron, stone, wood), never on polished metal or skin.
- **Dithering:** optional, and only on large flat surfaces. Avoid it on small or animated sprites.

---

## 6. Master palette

Today the 9 sprites use **81 unique colors**, and the loaded palette (*DawnBringer 32 copy*) is barely used. There are also duplicates the eye cannot tell apart (`#000000`/`#010101`, `#434848`/`#434846`, and six different whites). The proposal consolidates them into fixed ramps, starting from the colors already in use.

### Neutrals and outline
| Use | Colors |
|---|---|
| Outline | `#000000` |
| Specular white (the only one) | `#F0F0F0` |

### Iron / steel (shield, axe, sword)
`#262827` â†’ `#373837` â†’ `#434848` â†’ `#555957` â†’ `#758685` â†’ `#9DAAA9` â†’ `#B8C3BD` â†’ `#E5DCDC`

- Old dark iron (shield, axe): the first 5 tones, with `#B8C3BD` as the highlight.
- Polished steel (sword): from `#758685` to `#E5DCDC`.
- The axe blade uses warm greys (`#2F2A2A`, `#544F49`, `#B7B3AF`). Move it to this ramp, or create a "warm iron" ramp and document it.

### Obsidian (shaft of the magic staffs)
`#1D1D25` â†’ `#252734` â†’ `#32343C` â†’ `#655478` (light)

### Wood
`#41290D` â†’ `#57220D` â†’ `#6C4B24` â†’ `#8D2B11` â†’ `#926806`

The axe's wood (neutral brown) and `baston1`'s (reddish brown) differ today. Pick one direction and use it on both.

### Bone / ivory (sword guard, wings)
`#85836A` â†’ `#A19D4F` â†’ `#E2DE9C` â†’ `#E6E4B3` â†’ `#F5F3CF`

### Skin
`#F9C990` â†’ `#FBD6AA` â†’ `#EFD9BF`

A skin shadow tone is missing. Add a warm, slightly pink one, around `#C88A6A`.

### Elemental accents (the only saturated colors)
| Element | Ramp | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Blood / red | `#4B0A0A` â†’ `#810505` â†’ `#B70000` â†’ `#FF0000` | sword fuller, axe gem, Valki's hair |
| Fire | `#4B0F0F` â†’ `#BC2A12` â†’ `#F03416` â†’ `#F3610B` | `baston3` |
| Arcane / void | `#441974` â†’ `#650075` â†’ `#8548C0` â†’ `#B83ECC` â†’ `#C0A2E1` | `baston1.2`, shield gems |
| Ice | `#1C8B9B` â†’ `#14AFB4` â†’ `#00F6FF` | `baston2` |
| Valki's armor | `#00168A` â†’ `#384692` â†’ `#717694` | Valki |

About **50 colors** in total, against 81 today, each with a clear role. Save them as the project's own palette in Pixelorama and stop using DawnBringer 32 as the reference palette, since it is not the one actually in use.

### Color budget per sprite
- Weapons and items: up to 16 colors.
- Characters: up to 24 colors.
- At most 1 or 2 accent ramps per sprite.

---

## 7. Design language by family

### Staffs (variant system)
A very good system already exists; this formalizes it:

| Variant | Shaft | Head | Reads as |
|---|---|---|---|
| `baston1` | reddish wood | silver orb | basic / mundane |
| `baston1.2` | obsidian | arcane orb + floating particles | void / shadow |
| `baston2` | obsidian | ice orb | ice |
| `baston3` | obsidian + lava veins | fire orb | fire |

**Rule:** the base silhouette (22Ã—90 px) stays; only the accent ramp changes. The loose particles of `baston1.2` and the veins of `baston3` are a good marker of "magic level". Use them consistently: the basic variant has no effects, the elemental ones have veins or particles.

### Metal weapons
Dark iron, black outline and a single red accent (gem or fuller). The sword and the axe already follow this.

### Shields
A round iron base with textured wear and a raised cross. The ornate version adds arcane gems and a wooden strap. Future variants change the ornaments, not the base.

### Characters
Valki sets the reference: armor in a solid saturated color, hair as the main accent, and secondary elements (wings) in muted ivory so they do not compete with the body.

---

## 8. What we never do

- Colors outside the master palette.
- Automatic anti-aliasing, semi-transparency, blur or smooth gradients.
- Pure white `#FFFFFF` or stray whites. Only `#F0F0F0` exists.
- Saturated accents on large surfaces (an accent is small and pinpoint).
- Black and non-black outlines mixed inside one family.
- Light from different directions on sprites shown together.
- Texture noise on polished metal or skin.

---

## 9. Current inconsistencies and actions

| Problem | Files | Action |
|---|---|---|
| 81 colors, most outside DB32 | all | Remap to the master palette |
| Six near-identical whites | `valki`, `spada`, `baston*` | Unify on `#F0F0F0` |
| `#010101` instead of `#000000` | `axe` | Replace with `#000000` |
| `#434846` duplicating `#434848` | shields | Replace with `#434848` |
| Staffs without a black outline | `baston*` | Decide the rule (section 4) |
| Axe touching the edge (99 px) | `axe` | Shrink or re-center with a 2 px margin |
| Two different wood tones | `axe`, `baston1` | Unify the ramp |
| Warm greys on the axe | `axe` | Move to the iron ramp or document them |
| No saved palette | `baston1.2`, `baston2`, `baston3` | Assign the project palette |

---

## 10. Checklist before a sprite is done

- [ ] 100Ã—100 canvas, longest side 88â€“96 px, 2 px margin.
- [ ] Silhouette readable in pure black.
- [ ] 1 px outer outline following its family's rule.
- [ ] Light from the top left.
- [ ] Every color is in the master palette.
- [ ] Within the color budget (16 for items, 24 for characters).
- [ ] At most 1 or 2 saturated accents.
- [ ] No semi-transparency.
- [ ] Next to the other sprites of its family, it looks like the same game.

 147: 		}
 148: 		if (QuadMaterial)
 149: 		{
 150: 			Quad->SetMaterial(0, QuadMaterial);
 151: 		}
 152: 	}
 153: 	RebuildLight();
 154: }
 155: 
 156: void UGBPixelVfxComponent::RebuildLight()
 157: {
 158: 	const bool bWantsLight = CurrentClip.IsValid() && CurrentClip->Light.bEnabled && bEnableLight;
 159: 	if (!bWantsLight)
 160: 	{
 161: 		if (ClipLight)
 162: 		{
 163: 			ClipLight->DestroyComponent();
 164: 			ClipLight = nullptr;
 165: 		}
 166: 		return;
 167: 	}
 168: 	if (!ClipLight)
 169: 	{
 170: 		ClipLight = NewObject<UPointLightComponent>(GetOwner() ? static_cast<UObject*>(GetOwner()) : static_cast<UObject*>(this), NAME_None, RF_Transient);
 171: 		ClipLight->SetMobility(EComponentMobility::Movable);
 172: 		ClipLight->SetIntensityUnits(ELightUnits::Candelas);
 173: 		ClipLight->AttachToComponent(this, FAttachmentTransformRules::KeepRelativeTransform);
 174: 		ClipLight->RegisterComponent();
 175: 	}
 176: 	ClipLight->SetLightColor(CurrentClip->Light.Color);
 177: 	ClipLight->SetAttenuationRadius(CurrentClip->Light.RadiusCm * FMath::Max(0.01f, SizeScale));
 178: 	ClipLight->SetCastShadows(bLightCastsShadows);
 179: }
 180: 
 181: void UGBPixelVfxComponent::UpdateFrame(bool bForce)
 182: {
 183: 	if (!bPlaying || !CurrentClip.IsValid())
 184: 	{
 185: 		return;
 186: 	}
 187: 	bool bOutFinished = false;
 188: 	const int32 Frame = FGBPixelVfxClip::FrameAtTime(*CurrentClip, PlayTime, bOutFinished);
 189: 	if (bOutFinished)
 190: 	{
 191: 		Stop();
 192: 		if (bAutoDestroyOwnerWhenFinished && GetOwner())
 193: 		{
 194: 			GetOwner()->Destroy();
 195: 		}
 196: 		return;
 197: 	}
 198: 	if (Frame == CurrentFrame && !bForce)
 199: 	{
 200: 		return;
 201: 	}
 202: 	CurrentFrame = Frame;
 203: 	const FVector4f Rect = FGBPixelVfxClip::FrameRect(*CurrentClip, Frame);
 204: 	if (QuadMaterial)
 205: 	{
 206: 		// The frame rect rides as four scalars (see make_pixelvfx_material.py): masks on a
 207: 		// uniform that feeds the texture UVs fail to compile, so there is no vector swizzle.
 208: 		// The Plane mesh is assumed to run U along +X and V along +Y with V = 0 at the bottom;
 209: 		// UE samples (0, 0) at the image top-left, so V is flipped here.
 210: 		QuadMaterial->SetScalarParameterValue(TEXT("FrameU"), Rect.X);
 211: 		QuadMaterial->SetScalarParameterValue(TEXT("FrameV"), Rect.Y + Rect.W);
 212: 		QuadMaterial->SetScalarParameterValue(TEXT("FrameW"), Rect.Z);
 213: 		QuadMaterial->SetScalarParameterValue(TEXT("FrameH"), -Rect.W);
 214: 	}
 215: 	if (ClipLight && CurrentClip->Light.bEnabled)
 216: 	{
 217: 		float TrackValue = 1.f;
 218: 		if (CurrentClip->Light.TrackMode.Equals(TEXT("Constant"), ESearchCase::IgnoreCase)
 219: 			|| CurrentClip->Light.Track.Num() != CurrentClip->FrameCount)
 220: 		{
 221: 			TrackValue = 1.f;
 222: 		}
 223: 		else if (CurrentClip->Light.Track.IsValidIndex(Frame))
 224: 		{
 225: 			TrackValue = CurrentClip->Light.Track[Frame];
 226: 		}
 227: 		ClipLight->SetIntensity(CurrentClip->Light.Intensity * TrackValue * LightIntensityScale);
 228: 	}
 229: }
 230: 
 231: FVector UGBPixelVfxComponent::PivotWorldOffset() const
 232: {
 233: 	// Quad-local position of the pivot point; the quad is shifted so it lands on the component.
 234: 	if (!CurrentClip.IsValid())
 235: 	{
 236: 		return FVector::ZeroVector;
 237: 	}
 238: 	const float W = CurrentClip->WorldWidthCm * SizeScale;
 239: 	const float H = CurrentClip->WorldHeightCm * SizeScale;
 240: 	return FVector((CurrentClip->PivotU - 0.5f) * W, (0.5f - CurrentClip->PivotV) * H, 0.f);
 241: }
 242: 
 243: void UGBPixelVfxComponent::UpdateQuadTransform()
 244: {
 245: 	if (!Quad || !CurrentClip.IsValid())
 246: 	{
 247: 		return;
 248: 	}
 249: 	const float W = CurrentClip->WorldWidthCm * SizeScale;
 250: 	const float H = CurrentClip->WorldHeightCm * SizeScale;
 251: 	Quad->SetRelativeScale3D(FVector(FMath::Max(0.01f, W / 100.f), FMath::Max(0.01f, H / 100.f), 1.f));
 252: 	Quad->SetRelativeLocation(-PivotWorldOffset());
 253: 
 254: 	EGBPixelVfxFacing Facing = CurrentClip->Facing;
 255: 	if (!FacingOverride.IsNone())
 256: 	{
 257: 		const FString Text = FacingOverride.ToString();
 258: 		if (Text.Equals(TEXT("Billboard"), ESearchCase::IgnoreCase)) Facing = EGBPixelVfxFacing::Billboard;
 259: 		else if (Text.Equals(TEXT("Upright"), ESearchCase::IgnoreCase)) Facing = EGBPixelVfxFacing::Upright;
 260: 		else if (Text.Equals(TEXT("Ground"), ESearchCase::IgnoreCase)) Facing = EGBPixelVfxFacing::Ground;
 261: 		else if (Text.Equals(TEXT("Fixed"), ESearchCase::IgnoreCase)) Facing = EGBPixelVfxFacing::Fixed;
 262: 	}
 263: 
 264: 	if (Facing == EGBPixelVfxFacing::Fixed)
 265: 	{
 266: 		Quad->SetRelativeRotation(FRotator::ZeroRotator);
 267: 	}
 268: 	else if (Facing == EGBPixelVfxFacing::Ground)
 269: 	{
 270: 		Quad->SetRelativeRotation(FRotator::ZeroRotator);
 271: 	}
 272: 	else
 273: 	{
 274: 		FVector CamPos = FVector::ZeroVector;
 275: 		FVector CamUp = FVector::UpVector;
 276: 		bool bHasCamera = false;
 277: 		if (UWorld* World = GetWorld())
 278: 		{
 279: 			if (APlayerCameraManager* Manager = UGameplayStatics::GetPlayerCameraManager(World, 0))
 280: 			{
 281: 				CamPos = Manager->GetCameraLocation();
 282: 				CamUp = Manager->GetCameraRotation().RotateVector(FVector::UpVector);
 283: 				bHasCamera = true;
 284: 			}
 285: 		}
 286: 		if (bHasCamera)
 287: 		{
 288: 			const FVector QuadPos = Quad->GetComponentLocation();
 289: 			FVector ToCam = CamPos - QuadPos;
 290: 			if (Facing == EGBPixelVfxFacing::Upright)
 291: 			{
 292: 				ToCam.Z = 0.f;
 293: 				CamUp = FVector::UpVector;
 294: 			}
 295: 			if (ToCam.SizeSquared() > 1.f)
 296: 			{
 297: 				const FVector ZAxis = ToCam.GetSafeNormal();
 298: 				FVector XAxis = FVector::CrossProduct(CamUp, ZAxis);
 299: 				if (XAxis.SizeSquared() < 1.e-6f)
 300: 				{
 301: 					XAxis = FVector(1.f, 0.f, 0.f);
 302: 				}
 303: 				else
 304: 				{
 305: 					XAxis.Normalize();
 306: 				}
 307: 				const FVector YAxis = FVector::CrossProduct(ZAxis, XAxis);
 308: 				Quad->SetWorldRotation(FMatrix(XAxis, YAxis, ZAxis, FVector::ZeroVector).Rotator());
 309: 			}
 310: 		}
 311: 	}
 312: 
 313: 	if (ClipLight && CurrentClip.IsValid())
 314: 	{
 315: 		const float LW = CurrentClip->WorldWidthCm * SizeScale;
 316: 		const float LH = CurrentClip->WorldHeightCm * SizeScale;
 317: 		ClipLight->SetRelativeLocation(FVector(
 318: 			(CurrentClip->Light.AnchorU - CurrentClip->PivotU) * LW,
 319: 			(CurrentClip->PivotV - CurrentClip->Light.AnchorV) * LH,
 320: 			0.f));
 321: 	}
 322: }
 323: 
 324: void UGBPixelVfxComponent::TickComponent(float DeltaTime, ELevelTick TickType,
 325: 	FActorComponentTickFunction* ThisTickFunction)
 326: {
 327: 	Super::TickComponent(DeltaTime, TickType, ThisTickFunction);
 328: 	if (!bPlaying || !CurrentClip.IsValid())
 329: 	{
 330: 		return;
 331: 	}
 332: 	PlayTime += DeltaTime * PlayRate;
 333: 	UpdateFrame(false);
 334: 	UpdateQuadTransform();
 335: }
 336: 
 337: void UGBPixelVfxComponent::GetQuadWorldBounds(FVector& OutOrigin, FVector& OutExtent) const
 338: {
 339: 	OutOrigin = FVector::ZeroVector;
 340: 	OutExtent = FVector::ZeroVector;
 341: 	if (Quad && Quad->GetStaticMesh())
 342: 	{
 343: 		const FBoxSphereBounds MeshBounds = Quad->GetStaticMesh()->GetBounds();
 344: 		OutOrigin = Quad->GetComponentTransform().TransformPosition(MeshBounds.Origin);
 345: 		OutExtent = Quad->GetComponentTransform().TransformVector(MeshBounds.BoxExtent);
 346: 	}
 347: }

   2: #include "GBPixelVfxLog.h"

  18: #if !UE_BUILD_SHIPPING
  19: static void PixelVfxSpawnCommand(const TArray<FString>& Args, UWorld* World)
  20: {
  21: 	if (!World)
  22: 	{
  23: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("pvfx.Spawn: no world."));
  24: 		return;
  25: 	}
  26: 	if (Args.Num() < 1)
  27: 	{
  28: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("pvfx.Spawn usage: pvfx.Spawn <ClipId> [SizeScale]"));
  29: 		return;
  30: 	}
  31: 	const FName ClipId(*Args[0]);
  32: 	float SizeScale = 1.f;
  33: 	if (Args.Num() > 1)
  34: 	{
  35: 		SizeScale = FCString::Atof(*Args[1]);
  36: 	}
  37: 	APlayerCameraManager* Manager = UGameplayStatics::GetPlayerCameraManager(World, 0);
  38: 	if (!Manager)
  39: 	{
  40: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("pvfx.Spawn: no player camera."));
  41: 		return;
  42: 	}
  43: 	const FVector ViewPos = Manager->GetCameraLocation();
  44: 	const FVector ViewDir = Manager->GetCameraRotation().Vector();
  45: 	const FVector Point = ViewPos + ViewDir * 300.f;
  46: 	FVector SpawnAt = Point;
  47: 	FHitResult Hit;
  48: 	FCollisionQueryParams Params(SCENE_QUERY_STAT(GBPixelVfxSpawn), false);
  49: 	if (World->LineTraceSingleByChannel(Hit, Point, Point - FVector(0.f, 0.f, 2000.f), ECC_Visibility, Params))
  50: 	{
  51: 		SpawnAt = Hit.Location;
  52: 	}
  53: 	if (!AGBPixelVfxActor::SpawnPixelVfx(World, ClipId, FTransform(SpawnAt), SizeScale))
  54: 	{
  55: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("pvfx.Spawn: unknown clip '%s'."), *ClipId.ToString());
  56: 	}
  57: }
  58:

  62: 	FConsoleCommandWithWorldAndArgsDelegate::CreateStatic(&PixelVfxSpawnCommand));

C:\GameJam\Glyphbound\Plugins\GBPixelVfx\Source\GBPixelVfx\Private\GBPixelVfxSubsystem.cpp
   1: // UGBPixelVfxSubsystem: clip JSON + PNG atlas loading with caching.
   2: #include "GBPixelVfxSubsystem.h"
   3: 
   4: #include "Engine/Texture2D.h"
   5: #include "HAL/FileManager.h"
   6: #include "ImageCore.h"
   7: #include "ImageUtils.h"
   8: #include "Misc/FileHelper.h"
   9: #include "Misc/Paths.h"
  10: #include "Modules/ModuleManager.h"
  11: 
  12: FString UGBPixelVfxSubsystem::VfxDataDir()
  13: {
  14: 	return FPaths::ProjectContentDir() / TEXT("Data/Vfx");
  15: }
  16: 
  17: bool UGBPixelVfxSubsystem::LoadClip(FName Id) const
  18: {
  19: 	if (Id.IsNone())
  20: 	{
  21: 		return false;
  22: 	}
  23: 	if (ClipCache.Contains(Id) || MissingIds.Contains(Id))
  24: 	{
  25: 		return ClipCache.Contains(Id);
  26: 	}
  27: 	const FString JsonPath = VfxDataDir() / (Id.ToString() + TEXT(".json"));
  28: 	FString JsonText;
  29: 	if (!FFileHelper::LoadFileToString(JsonText, *JsonPath))
  30: 	{
  31: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("PixelVfx clip '%s' is missing (%s)."), *Id.ToString(), *JsonPath);
  32: 		MissingIds.Add(Id);
  33: 		return false;
  34: 	}
  35: 	FGBPixelVfxClip Clip;
  36: 	if (!Clip.LoadFromJsonString(JsonText))
  37: 	{
  38: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("PixelVfx clip '%s' is broken (%s)."), *Id.ToString(), *JsonPath);
  39: 		MissingIds.Add(Id);
  40: 		return false;
  41: 	}
  42: 	ClipCache.Add(Id, MoveTemp(Clip));
  43: 	return true;
  44: }
  45: 
  46: const FGBPixelVfxClip* UGBPixelVfxSubsystem::FindClip(FName Id) const
  47: {
  48: 	if (!LoadClip(Id))
  49: 	{
  50: 		return nullptr;
  51: 	}
  52: 	return ClipCache.Find(Id);
  53: }
  54: 
  55: UTexture2D* UGBPixelVfxSubsystem::GetAtlas(FName Id) const
  56: {
  57: 	const FGBPixelVfxClip* Clip = FindClip(Id);
  58: 	if (!Clip)
  59: 	{
  60: 		return nullptr;
  61: 	}
  62: 	if (TObjectPtr<UTexture2D>* Cached = AtlasCache.Find(Id))
  63: 	{
  64: 		if (*Cached)
  65: 		{
  66: 			return *Cached;
  67: 		}
  68: 	}
  69: 	const FString PngPath = VfxDataDir() / Clip->AtlasFile;
  70: 	TArray<uint8> PngBytes;
  71: 	if (!FFileHelper::LoadFileToArray(PngBytes, *PngPath))
  72: 	{
  73: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("PixelVfx atlas for '%s' is missing (%s)."), *Id.ToString(), *PngPath);
  74: 		MissingIds.Add(Id);
  75: 		ClipCache.Remove(Id);
  76: 		return nullptr;
  77: 	}
  78: 	FImage Image;
  79: 	if (!FImageUtils::DecompressImage(PngBytes.GetData(), PngBytes.Num(), Image)
  80: 		|| Image.Format != ERawImageFormat::BGRA8)
  81: 	{
  82: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("PixelVfx atlas for '%s' could not be decoded."), *Id.ToString());
  83: 		return nullptr;
  84: 	}
  85: 	const int32 AtlasW = Image.SizeX;
  86: 	const int32 AtlasH = Image.SizeY;
  87: 	const int32 ExpectW = Clip->Columns * Clip->FrameWidth;
  88: 	const int32 ExpectH = Clip->Rows() * Clip->FrameHeight;
  89: 	if (AtlasW != ExpectW || AtlasH != ExpectH
  90: 		|| Image.RawData.Num() != static_cast<int64>(AtlasW) * AtlasH * 4)
  91: 	{
  92: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("PixelVfx atlas for '%s' is %dx%d but the clip needs %dx%d."),
  93: 			*Id.ToString(), AtlasW, AtlasH, ExpectW, ExpectH);
  94: 		return nullptr;
  95: 	}
  96: 	UTexture2D* Texture = UTexture2D::CreateTransient(AtlasW, AtlasH, PF_B8G8R8A8);
  97: 	if (!Texture || !Texture->GetPlatformData() || Texture->GetPlatformData()->Mips.Num() == 0)
  98: 	{
  99: 		UE_LOG(LogGBPixelVfx, Warning, TEXT("PixelVfx atlas for '%s': could not create a transient texture."), *Id.ToString());
 100: 		return nullptr;
 101: 	}
 102: 	Texture->Filter = TF_Nearest;
 103: 	Texture->SRGB = true;
 104: 	Texture->NeverStream = true;
 105: 	Texture->CompressionSettings = TC_EditorIcon;
 106: 	Texture->LODGroup = TEXTUREGROUP_Pixels2D;
 107: 	Texture->AddressX = TA_Clamp;
 108: 	Texture->AddressY = TA_Clamp;
 109: #if WITH_EDITORONLY_DATA
 110: 	Texture->MipGenSettings = TMGS_NoMipmaps;
 111: #endif
 112: 	// FColor is B, G, R, A in memory: exactly BGRA8; rows go top to bottom.
 113: 	FTexture2DMipMap& Mip = Texture->GetPlatformData()->Mips[0];
 114: 	void* Data = Mip.BulkData.Lock(LOCK_READ_WRITE);
 115: 	FMemory::Memcpy(Data, Image.RawData.GetData(), Image.RawData.Num());
 116: 	Mip.BulkData.Unlock();
 117: 	Texture->UpdateResource();
 118: 	AtlasCache.Add(Id, Texture);
 119: 	return Texture;
 120: }
 121: 
 122: TArray<FName> UGBPixelVfxSubsystem::ListClipIds() const
 123: {
 124: 	TArray<FName> Ids;
 125: 	TArray<FString> Files;
 126: 	IFileManager::Get().FindFiles(Files, *(VfxDataDir() / TEXT("*.json")), true, false);
 127: 	for (const FString& File : Files)
 128: 	{
 129: 		Ids.Add(FName(*FPaths::GetBaseFilename(File)));
 130: 	}
 131: 	Ids.Sort([](const FName& A, const FName& B) { return A.LexicalLess(B); });
 132: 	return Ids;
 133: }

 135: void UGBPixelVfxSubsystem::ClearCache()
 136: {
 137: 	AtlasCache.Reset();
 138: 	ClipCache.Reset();
 139: 	MissingIds.Reset();
 140: }
C:\GameJam\Glyphbound\Plugins\GBPixelVfx\Source\GBPixelVfx\Private\GBPixelVfxTypes.cpp
   1: // FGBPixelVfxClip: JSON read/write plus the pure playback helpers (unit-testable, no engine objects).
   2: #include "GBPixelVfxTypes.h"
   3: 
   4: #include "Dom/JsonObject.h"
   5: #include "Dom/JsonValue.h"
   6: #include "Serialization/JsonReader.h"
   7: #include "Serialization/JsonSerializer.h"
   8: 
   9: namespace GBPixelVfxClipLocal
  10: {
  11: 	EGBPixelVfxFacing FacingFromString(const FString& Text)
  12: 	{
  13: 		if (Text.Equals(TEXT("Billboard"), ESearchCase::IgnoreCase))
  14: 		{
  15: 			return EGBPixelVfxFacing::Billboard;
  16: 		}
  17: 		if (Text.Equals(TEXT("Ground"), ESearchCase::IgnoreCase))
  18: 		{
  19: 			return EGBPixelVfxFacing::Ground;
  20: 		}
  21: 		if (Text.Equals(TEXT("Fixed"), ESearchCase::IgnoreCase))
  22: 		{
  23: 			return EGBPixelVfxFacing::Fixed;
  24: 		}
  25: 		return EGBPixelVfxFacing::Upright;
  26: 	}
  27: 
  28: 	FString FacingToString(EGBPixelVfxFacing Facing)
  29: 	{
  30: 		switch (Facing)
  31: 		{
  32: 		case EGBPixelVfxFacing::Billboard: return TEXT("Billboard");
  33: 		case EGBPixelVfxFacing::Ground: return TEXT("Ground");
  34: 		case EGBPixelVfxFacing::Fixed: return TEXT("Fixed");
  35: 		default: return TEXT("Upright");
  36: 		}
  37: 	}
  38: 
  39: 	int32 HexDigit(TCHAR C)
  40: 	{
  41: 		if (C >= TEXT('0') && C <= TEXT('9')) return C - TEXT('0');
  42: 		if (C >= TEXT('a') && C <= TEXT('f')) return 10 + C - TEXT('a');
  43: 		if (C >= TEXT('A') && C <= TEXT('F')) return 10 + C - TEXT('A');
  44: 		return -1;
  45: 	}
  46: 
  47: 	// "#RRGGBB" (the '#' is optional) -> linear color. Returns white when the text is broken.
  48: 	FLinearColor ColorFromHex(const FString& Hex)
  49: 	{
  50: 		FString Text = Hex;
  51: 		Text.TrimStartAndEndInline();
  52: 		if (Text.StartsWith(TEXT("#")))
  53: 		{
  54: 			Text.RightChopInline(1);
  55: 		}
  56: 		if (Text.Len() != 6)
  57: 		{
  58: 			return FLinearColor::White;
  59: 		}
  60: 		uint8 Bytes[3] = { 255, 255, 255 };
  61: 		for (int32 i = 0; i < 3; ++i)
  62: 		{
  63: 			const int32 Hi = HexDigit(Text[i * 2]);
  64: 			const int32 Lo = HexDigit(Text[i * 2 + 1]);
  65: 			if (Hi < 0 || Lo < 0)
  66: 			{
  67: 				return FLinearColor::White;
  68: 			}
  69: 			Bytes[i] = static_cast<uint8>(Hi * 16 + Lo);
  70: 		}
  71: 		return FLinearColor(FColor(Bytes[0], Bytes[1], Bytes[2]));
  72: 	}
  73: 
  74: 	FString ColorToHex(const FLinearColor& Color)
  75: 	{
  76: 		const FColor Srgb = Color.ToFColor(true);
  77: 		return FString::Printf(TEXT("#%02X%02X%02X"), Srgb.R, Srgb.G, Srgb.B);
  78: 	}
  79: }
  80: 
  81: FVector4f FGBPixelVfxClip::FrameRect(const FGBPixelVfxClip& Clip, int32 FrameIndex)
  82: {
  83: 	if (Clip.Columns <= 0 || Clip.FrameWidth <= 0 || Clip.FrameHeight <= 0 || Clip.Rows() <= 0)
  84: 	{
  85: 		return FVector4f(0.f, 0.f, 1.f, 1.f);
  86: 	}
  87: 	const int32 Clamped = FMath::Clamp(FrameIndex, 0, Clip.FrameCount - 1);
  88: 	const float AtlasW = static_cast<float>(Clip.Columns * Clip.FrameWidth);
  89: 	const float AtlasH = static_cast<float>(Clip.Rows() * Clip.FrameHeight);
  90: 	const int32 Col = Clamped % Clip.Columns;
  91: 	const int32 Row = Clamped / Clip.Columns;
  92: 	return FVector4f(
  93: 		Col * Clip.FrameWidth / AtlasW,
  94: 		Row * Clip.FrameHeight / AtlasH,
  95: 		Clip.FrameWidth / AtlasW,
  96: 		Clip.FrameHeight / AtlasH);
  97: }
  98: 
  99: int32 FGBPixelVfxClip::FrameAtTime(const FGBPixelVfxClip& Clip, float Seconds, bool& bOutFinished)
 100: {
 101: 	bOutFinished = false;
 102: 	if (Clip.FrameCount <= 0 || Clip.Fps <= 0)
 103: 	{
 104: 		bOutFinished = true;
 105: 		return 0;
 106: 	}
 107: 	const int32 Stepped = FMath::FloorToInt(Seconds * static_cast<float>(Clip.Fps));
 108: 	if (Clip.bLoop)
 109: 	{
 110: 		const int32 Wrapped = Stepped % Clip.FrameCount;
 111: 		return Wrapped >= 0 ? Wrapped : Wrapped + Clip.FrameCount;
 112: 	}
 113: 	if (Stepped >= Clip.FrameCount)
 114: 	{
 115: 		bOutFinished = true;
 116: 		return Clip.FrameCount - 1;
 117: 	}
 118: 	return FMath::Max(0, Stepped);
 119: }
 120: 
 121: bool FGBPixelVfxClip::LoadFromJsonString(const FString& Text)
 122: {
 123: 	TSharedPtr<FJsonObject> Root;
 124: 	const TSharedRef<TJsonReader<>> Reader = TJsonReaderFactory<>::Create(Text);
 125: 	if (!FJsonSerializer::Deserialize(Reader, Root) || !Root.IsValid())
 126: 	{
 127: 		return false;
 128: 	}
 129: 
 130: 	FGBPixelVfxClip Fresh;
 131: 	FString IdText;
 132: 	if (Root->TryGetStringField(TEXT("Id"), IdText))
 133: 	{
 134: 		Fresh.Id = FName(*IdText);
 135: 	}
 136: 	Root->TryGetStringField(TEXT("Atlas"), Fresh.AtlasFile);
 137: 	Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("FrameWidth"), Fresh.FrameWidth);
 138: 	Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("FrameHeight"), Fresh.FrameHeight);
 139: 	Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("Columns"), Fresh.Columns);
 140: 	Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("FrameCount"), Fresh.FrameCount);
 141: 	Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("Fps"), Fresh.Fps);
 142: 	Fresh.FrameWidth = FMath::Max(0, Fresh.FrameWidth);
 143: 	Fresh.FrameHeight = FMath::Max(0, Fresh.FrameHeight);
 144: 	Fresh.Columns = FMath::Max(0, Fresh.Columns);
 145: 	Fresh.FrameCount = FMath::Max(0, Fresh.FrameCount);
 146: 	Fresh.Fps = FMath::Max(0, Fresh.Fps);
 147: 	Root->TryGetBoolField(TEXT("Loop"), Fresh.bLoop);
 148: 
 149: 	double WorldW = 0.0;
 150: 	double WorldH = 0.0;
 151: 	if (Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("WorldWidthCm"), WorldW))
 152: 	{
 153: 		Fresh.WorldWidthCm = static_cast<float>(WorldW);
 154: 	}
 155: 	if (Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("WorldHeightCm"), WorldH))
 156: 	{
 157: 		Fresh.WorldHeightCm = static_cast<float>(WorldH);
 158: 	}
 159: 	double PivotUVal = 0.5;
 160: 	double PivotVVal = 0.5;
 161: 	if (Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("PivotU"), PivotUVal))
 162: 	{
 163: 		Fresh.PivotU = static_cast<float>(PivotUVal);
 164: 	}
 165: 	if (Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("PivotV"), PivotVVal))
 166: 	{
 167: 		Fresh.PivotV = static_cast<float>(PivotVVal);
 168: 	}
 169: 
 170: 	FString FacingText;
 171: 	if (Root->TryGetStringField(TEXT("Facing"), FacingText))
 172: 	{
 173: 		Fresh.Facing = GBPixelVfxClipLocal::FacingFromString(FacingText);
 174: 	}
 175: 	double GlowVal = 0.0;
 176: 	if (Root->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("Glow"), GlowVal))
 177: 	{
 178: 		Fresh.Glow = static_cast<float>(GlowVal);
 179: 	}
 180: 	Root->TryGetBoolField(TEXT("Lit"), Fresh.bLit);
 181: 
 182: 	const TSharedPtr<FJsonObject>* LightField = nullptr;
 183: 	if (Root->TryGetObjectField(TEXT("Light"), LightField) && LightField && LightField->IsValid())
 184: 	{
 185: 		const TSharedPtr<FJsonObject>& LightJson = *LightField;
 186: 		LightJson->TryGetBoolField(TEXT("Enabled"), Fresh.Light.bEnabled);
 187: 		if (LightJson->TryGetStringField(TEXT("Color"), Fresh.Light.ColorHex))
 188: 		{
 189: 			Fresh.Light.Color = GBPixelVfxClipLocal::ColorFromHex(Fresh.Light.ColorHex);
 190: 		}
 191: 		double Intensity = 0.0;
 192: 		if (LightJson->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("Intensity"), Intensity))
 193: 		{
 194: 			Fresh.Light.Intensity = static_cast<float>(Intensity);
 195: 		}
 196: 		double Radius = 0.0;
 197: 		if (LightJson->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("RadiusCm"), Radius))
 198: 		{
 199: 			Fresh.Light.RadiusCm = static_cast<float>(Radius);
 200: 		}
 201: 		LightJson->TryGetBoolField(TEXT("CastShadows"), Fresh.Light.bCastShadows);
 202: 		double AnchorU = 0.5;
 203: 		double AnchorV = 0.5;
 204: 		if (LightJson->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("AnchorU"), AnchorU))
 205: 		{
 206: 			Fresh.Light.AnchorU = static_cast<float>(AnchorU);
 207: 		}
 208: 		if (LightJson->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("AnchorV"), AnchorV))
 209: 		{
 210: 			Fresh.Light.AnchorV = static_cast<float>(AnchorV);
 211: 		}
 212: 		LightJson->TryGetStringField(TEXT("TrackMode"), Fresh.Light.TrackMode);
 213: 		const TArray<TSharedPtr<FJsonValue>>* Track = nullptr;
 214: 		if (LightJson->TryGetArrayField(TEXT("Track"), Track) && Track)
 215: 		{
 216: 			Fresh.Light.Track.Reset(Track->Num());
 217: 			for (const TSharedPtr<FJsonValue>& Entry : *Track)
 218: 			{
 219: 				Fresh.Light.Track.Add(static_cast<float>(Entry->AsNumber()));
 220: 			}
 221: 		}
 222: 	}
 223: 
 224: 	const TSharedPtr<FJsonObject>* SourceObj = nullptr;
 225: 	if (Root->TryGetObjectField(TEXT("Source"), SourceObj) && SourceObj && SourceObj->IsValid())
 226: 	{
 227: 		const TSharedPtr<FJsonObject>& Source = *SourceObj;
 228: 		Source->TryGetStringField(TEXT("System"), Fresh.SourceSystem);
 229: 		double Duration = 0.0;
 230: 		if (Source->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("DurationSeconds"), Duration))
 231: 		{
 232: 			Fresh.SourceDurationSeconds = static_cast<float>(Duration);
 233: 		}
 234: 		Source->TryGetNumberField(TEXT("SimFps"), Fresh.SourceSimFps);
 235: 		Source->TryGetStringField(TEXT("EngineVersion"), Fresh.EngineVersion);
 236: 		Source->TryGetStringField(TEXT("BakedUtc"), Fresh.BakedUtc);
 237: 	}
 238: 
 239: 	if (Fresh.Id.IsNone() || Fresh.FrameCount <= 0 || Fresh.FrameWidth <= 0 || Fresh.FrameHeight <= 0
 240: 		|| Fresh.Columns <= 0 || Fresh.AtlasFile.IsEmpty())
 241: 	{
 242: 		return false;
 243: 	}
 244: 	*this = Fresh;
 245: 	return true;
 246: }
 247: 
 248: bool FGBPixelVfxClip::SaveToJsonString(FString& OutText) const
 249: {
 250: 	const TSharedRef<FJsonObject> Root = MakeShared<FJsonObject>();
 251: 	Root->SetStringField(TEXT("Id"), Id.ToString());
 252: 	Root->SetStringField(TEXT("Atlas"), AtlasFile);
 253: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("FrameWidth"), FrameWidth);
 254: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("FrameHeight"), FrameHeight);
 255: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("Columns"), Columns);
 256: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("FrameCount"), FrameCount);
 257: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("Fps"), Fps);
 258: 	Root->SetBoolField(TEXT("Loop"), bLoop);
 259: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("WorldWidthCm"), WorldWidthCm);
 260: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("WorldHeightCm"), WorldHeightCm);
 261: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("PivotU"), PivotU);
 262: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("PivotV"), PivotV);
 263: 	Root->SetStringField(TEXT("Facing"), GBPixelVfxClipLocal::FacingToString(Facing));
 264: 	Root->SetNumberField(TEXT("Glow"), Glow);
 265: 	Root->SetBoolField(TEXT("Lit"), bLit);
 266: 
 267: 	const TSharedRef<FJsonObject> LightJson = MakeShared<FJsonObject>();
 268: 	LightJson->SetBoolField(TEXT("Enabled"), Light.bEnabled);
 269: 	LightJson->SetStringField(TEXT("Color"), Light.ColorHex);
 270: 	LightJson->SetNumberField(TEXT("Intensity"), Light.Intensity);
 271: 	LightJson->SetNumberField(TEXT("RadiusCm"), Light.RadiusCm);
 272: 	LightJson->SetBoolField(TEXT("CastShadows"), Light.bCastShadows);
 273: 	LightJson->SetNumberField(TEXT("AnchorU"), Light.AnchorU);
 274: 	LightJson->SetNumberField(TEXT("AnchorV"), Light.AnchorV);
 275: 	LightJson->SetStringField(TEXT("TrackMode"), Light.TrackMode);
 276: 	TArray<TSharedPtr<FJsonValue>> TrackValues;
 277: 	for (float Entry : Light.Track)
 278: 	{
 279: 		TrackValues.Add(MakeShared<FJsonValueNumber>(Entry));
 280: 	}
 281: 	LightJson->SetArrayField(TEXT("Track"), TrackValues);
 282: 	Root->SetObjectField(TEXT("Light"), LightJson);
 283: 
 284: 	const TSharedRef<FJsonObject> Source = MakeShared<FJsonObject>();
 285: 	Source->SetStringField(TEXT("System"), SourceSystem);
 286: 	Source->SetNumberField(TEXT("DurationSeconds"), SourceDurationSeconds);
 287: 	Source->SetNumberField(TEXT("SimFps"), SourceSimFps);
 288: 	Source->SetStringField(TEXT("EngineVersion"), EngineVersion);
 289: 	Source->SetStringField(TEXT("BakedUtc"), BakedUtc);
 290: 	Root->SetObjectField(TEXT("Source"), Source);
 291: 
 292: 	const TSharedRef<TJsonWriter<>> Writer = TJsonWriterFactory<>::Create(&OutText);
 293: 	return FJsonSerializer::Serialize(Root, Writer);
 294: }

  17: 	// The Plane mesh is 100x100 cm in XY and faces +Z. Its TexCoord0 is assumed to run
  18: 	// U along +X and V along +Y with V = 0 at the bottom (-Y); UE samples textures with
  19: 	// (0, 0) at the image top-left, so V is flipped when the FrameRect is pushed below.

  21: 	const TCHAR* SpriteMaterialPath = TEXT("/Game/Glyphbound/Materials/M_PixelVfxSprite.M_PixelVfxSprite");
  22: 	const TCHAR* LitMaterialPath = TEXT("/Game/Glyphbound/Materials/M_PixelVfxSpriteLit.M_PixelVfxSpriteLit");

  26: UGBPixelVfxComponent::UGBPixelVfxComponent()
  27: {
  28: 	PrimaryComponentTick.bCanEverTick = true;
  29: 	PrimaryComponentTick.bStartWithTickEnabled = true;
